  recursive definitions (check_freevars, pat_bindings, pmatch, evaluate, etc.).

  Remaining 186 sorries

  The remaining sorries fall into hard categories that require substantial new
  infrastructure:

  1. Mutual induction over inductive predicates (type_p/type_ps,
  type_e/type_es/type_funs, type_v): ~30 sorries in TypeSysProps, Weakening,
  TypeSound. Lean 4's mutual induction tactic doesn't support these directly
  without manual eliminator construction.
  2. Deep substitution algebra over sem_t (e.g., type_subst_deBruijn_inc_single,
   type_subst_deBruijn_subst_single, deBruijn_subst2_thm, type_e_subst*): ~25
  sorries. Each needs the full sem_t.recOn with two motives plus carefully
  tracked nat arithmetic.
  3. Termination proofs (decreasing_by all_goals sorry): 11 sorries across
  check_freevars, pat_bindings, pmatch, evaluate, concrete_v, nsMap. Each needs
  sizeOf-based well-founded recursion on List _ for nested inductives.
  4. String byte/char position issues (Mlstring): 7 sorries depend on String.get
   s ⟨n⟩ semantics, which conflict with the iteration-by-character code
  structure for non-ASCII strings.
  5. Type soundness theorems (TypeSound.lean): 44 sorries — these are the main
  payoff theorems requiring all the upstream infrastructure.
  6. BIJ/CARD reasoning (Misc): 3 sorries on classical-choice based cardinality.
  7. nsLookup on nsAppend lemmas (NamespaceProps): 8 sorries depend on a missing
   lemma about ALOOKUP of list append; would need to derive that first.
  8. Complex is_clock_io_mono reasoning (EvaluateProps): 17 sorries about
  clock/IO state tracking through evaluator combinators.

  The remaining work largely consists of theorems that require building up
  infrastructure — once the substitution lemmas and mutual eliminators are in
  place, the dependent theorems would unblock.

✻ Churned for 25m 48s
